# The store: every product ranked, in six tiers and a miscellany

**Tier 0 added 2026-10-05/06: what costs nothing to start (his own
services, Bangalla dropship, Arbico dropship). It is what the Store page
shows; every tier is in the owner's dashboard under Store products.
Sections 0, 0.5, 2, 3, 3A, 4, 9, 10, 11 and appendix K changed.**

**Rebuilt 2026-09-28 (the day pass, second edition), on the measurements of
09-20, 09-27 and both passes of 09-28.** This is the one store document. Every
product row carries a link to that product on the supplier's own page, and
every link was opened in a browser during one of those passes; none was
constructed from a pattern. A price is a measurement with a date.

**How the tiers are ordered in this edition.** Within each tier the products
are **ranked, first to last, by how strongly they are recommended for the
store**: rank 1 is the line to stock first. The ranking weighs, in this order,
whether it fits his brief (non-liquid, garden, kitchen, games, soap), what it
costs to post (weight), whether it is bought again, whether the price is
public, how low the opening minimum is, and the trade signal (rating count,
country of origin). A rank is a recommendation, not a measurement, and the
reason is in the last column.

> His brief, in his words: *"Tier 1 products: garden fertilizers, pesticides,
> cleaning supplies (emphasis on eco-friendly, non liquids/concentrates),
> natural utensils and kitchenware, some of those family/kid games, soaps.
> Tier 2 could be items like garden tools, household items to replace plastic
> and such. Natural clothes/towels. More expensive items that I would have to
> store at home. Tier 3: is up to you and your research."* Plus: *"I do want
> the heavy DTE fertilizers at some point, maybe just to buy in person, so
> include a Tier 4 for Retail Store items"*, a **Tier 5** of everything worth a
> look later, and: *"Any products that may not fit in a tier then include a
> miscellaneous tier."*

## 0. How to read this

| Tier | What it is | Where it sells |
|---|---|---|
| **0** | **Costs nothing to start**: his own services, and products a supplier ships straight to the customer with no fee, no minimum and no stock. **What the Store page shows** | Online |
| **1** | The first stock, when there is money: the opening online order, light, no registration of his own, bought again | Online |
| **2** | The second round: heavier, dearer or slower, still postable | Online |
| **3** | The third round: gifts, collectables, serving pieces, seed gifts, bundles | Online |
| **4** | **Heavy, bulk and perishable**: the retail store, the stall, pickup, the supplier's truck | **Never posted** |
| **5** | The long list: everything researched that may fit later | Later |
| **Misc** | Real, read, priced, and in no tier: fixtures, commercial sizes, ingredients, impulse lines | Case by case |

**Price kinds, never mixed in a column.** **WHOLESALE**: a real case or unit
price from the supplier's page (Bangalla, Hummert, WebstaurantStore, Seven
Springs by the sack). **RETAIL**: the supplier's shelf price, the ceiling and
not his cost (Arbico, GrowOrganic, Frontier, Botanical Interests, Neptune's
Harvest, eeBoo, Dr. Earth, Seven Springs). **MSRP**: what Faire shows a
signed-out visitor; the wholesale price is behind the account, the opening
minimum is public ("no minimum shown" means the page printed none).
**LISTED**: Azure's member price. **HIDDEN**: needs an approved account.

**The day pass, first edition (2026-09-28 morning):** Fun Express is a toy and
novelty wholesaler, not the games shelf; BWI is the local heavy-goods route,
not the only route to fertiliser and pest control; Faire is a cleaning and
garden supplier when read by product rather than category page; Seventh
Generation and ECOS Pro have public case prices at WebstaurantStore; Espoma is
priced at Hummert; Bangalla stays.

**The day pass, second edition (2026-09-28 afternoon):** fifteen more
suppliers were opened for his categories (section 2), the register now says
for each what it sells, what it costs and how the store would use it, and
every tier is ranked. Found and worth an account: **Seven Springs Farm
Supply** (organic fertiliser by the 40 lb sack at farm prices, a retail-partner
programme), **High Mowing Organic Seeds** (a seed-rack programme with a public
order guide), **Rustic Strength** (a refill-shop wholesaler with 30 to 275
gallon sizes), **Fillaree** (closed-loop refill soap, a wholesale portal),
**Who Gives A Crap** (paper, a wholesale portal). Opened and NOT a fit, with
the reason: Made In Oklahoma Coalition (food and beverage only), Branch Basics
(affiliate programme only), Renee's Garden and Dr. Bronner's (brick-and-mortar
only), Baker Creek (no wholesale route on its site).

**Tier 0 (2026-10-05 and 06).** He has no capital for stock, so a Tier 0
was added: only what costs nothing to start. What made it possible: his own
Bangalla account download (21,901 rows), Bangalla's and Arbico's programme
pages re-read, and 59 of 59 public Bangalla pages agreeing with the file's
list prices. Found on the way: the price Bangalla shows the public is its
list price and his account pays less; most Bangalla household goods are
case-only; Bangalla has no games, seeds, dishcloths or bamboo tools, so
those stay in Tier 1. **His costs are not in this document, because the
repository is public on GitHub**; they are in the dashboard.

## 1. The decisions that shape every list

**The number that decides a category is the shipping weight, not the margin.**

| | Retail | Typical margin | Ship weight | Margin after a $9 parcel |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 lb box of blood meal | $18 | $7 | 5 lb | **under $1** |
| Bamboo toothbrush 4-pack | $12 | $6 | 4 oz | **$5** |
| 32 oz laundry powder | $17 | $7 | 2 lb | **$3** |
| Compost tumbler | $120 | $40 | 35 lb | **negative** unless the supplier ships it |

That is why Tier 4 exists, and why the rankings below put a $7 cloth above a
$20 sack. Seven settled decisions: **non-liquid first**; **a pesticide claim
is a registration** (the manufacturer's, $210 a label in Oklahoma; copy the
label and add nothing); **fertiliser is the same shape** ($100 a product, the
manufacturer's; the $50 licence is his); **nothing from China by parcel** (a
Faire page's "Made in: China" is the maker's import, already landed; it is
noted so he can weigh it); **the FTC thirty-day rule applies**; **check stock
before a product gets a page**; **a supplier's price list is not its shipping
list** (three DTE products cannot enter Oklahoma from Arbico).

### 2.2 Opened and not a fit, with the reason

**Added 2026-10-10 (AliExpress):** ranks 39 to 46 rebuilt from a page-by-page check on aliexpress.us. Dropped: the 80-cell trays (Nov delivery, a new-shopper price), the second plant labels, the mini tool set (4.6), the bamboo brushes (Full Circle covers them), the butter squishy (4.5) and the dancing cactus (does not ship to the US). AliExpress's terms bar copying its sellers' photos and text, so every AliExpress line gets our own photo from the sample order. The API keys work (2026-10-10); orders still need the account connected, the app approved, and each order paid in the AliExpress account unless auto-pay is approved (OWNER-STEPS G10).

**Added 2026-10-10:** **TeemDrop cannot be used**: it only fulfils orders for a store on one of its connected platforms (the owner, 2026-10-10), and lavishleaf.org is its own site. Its ten Tier 0 rows (ranks 30 to 38: beeswax wraps, bamboo cutlery and toothbrush, loofah scrubber, dish brush, dryer balls, straws, silicone bag, coconut bowl, compost bags, grow bags, plant labels, seedling box, weeding tool, self-watering pot, shampoo bar, frog light, mushroom lamp) are removed; AliExpress and Faire cover the same lines.

## 3. TIER 0, ranked: what costs nothing to start

**Added 2026-10-05, renamed Tier 0 on 2026-10-06, at his instruction:** *"the
products on our online store for this first stage needs to be products that
will cost me no money to start selling ... from dropshippers that can be
shipped directly to the [buyers] and cost me nothing to setup."* So a product
is in Tier 0 only if **all four** are true:

1. The supplier **ships it to the customer** (dropship). He never holds it.
2. **No fee to open or keep the account**, no minimum order, no per-order fee.
3. He pays the supplier **only after a customer has paid him**.
4. It is in stock and under the store's 10 lb posting limit.

Four suppliers pass: Bangalla and Arbico (re-read on their own pages on
2026-10-05), and TeemDrop and AliExpress Choice (2026-10-07, ranks 30 to 51):

* **Bangalla**: *"Setting up a wholesale account is free"*; *"we don't charge
  dropship or handling fees per order and there are no monthly minimums"*;
  *"No we do not require a minimum order"*; *"Payment for products and
  shipping are billed at the time the order placed"*
  ([dropshipping services](https://www.bangalla.com/dropshipping-services/)).
  The paid Gold level is optional and is not needed.
* **Arbico**: *"No additional dropship fees, no minimums, no case break fees
  and products are blind shipped to your customers"*
  ([distributor programme](https://www.arbico-organics.com/category/distributor-program-information)).
  The application is free; the discount comes with approval.

**Tier 0 is what the Store page shows** (`tools/demo-store.mjs` reads this
section). Tiers 1 to 4 and the Miscellany are in the owner's dashboard only,
under **Store products**, where he can compare them all.

### 3.1 The ranked list

**Ranks 1 to 19 are Bangalla (99 products) and can be
listed once the points in 3.2 are settled. Ranks 20 to 29 are
Arbico and wait only for the distributor application. Ranks 30 to 46
(added 2026-10-07, the owner: "add any teemdrop and AliExpress items ...
if any are a good fit") are TeemDrop and AliExpress Choice: free, no
minimum, shipped to the customer from China, so slower (8 to 20 days) and
every one needs a sample order before its page goes up. Ranks 47 to 51 (the same day, after GrowOrganic refused an online-only shop) are worm composting from two makers who dropship free, and four pest lines through Arbico, each checked as registered in Oklahoma. Their prices are
the supplier's cost, so this public file says "see page"; the TeemDrop links
are its catalogue search, and the SPU code in the Source column is the exact
item.**

**Where the Bangalla rows come from.** His own account download, `Bangalla
Product Data` (the Excel and the CSV, 21,901 rows each, 2026-10-01). Every
row here was in stock that day. **The price beside each name is Bangalla's
LIST price**, the one any visitor to bangalla.com sees: 59 of 59 public
product pages checked on 2026-10-05 showed exactly the file's list price,
and none said out of stock. **His own cost is lower, and it is deliberately
not written here**: this repository is public on GitHub. The dashboard's
**Store products** section shows cost, margin and stock for every row, read
from his file when he loads it there. The code in brackets is Bangalla's SKU,
which is how the dashboard matches the two. Where a row says some pages open
only when signed in, Bangalla refused a signed-out visitor; the product is
real and in his file.

**"Box" rows.** Bangalla sells most household goods by the case, not the
unit. A case can still be dropshipped, so those rows sell as what they are:
a box of 24 sponges, a box of 288 forks. They rank below the singles.

**Everything else Bangalla has in his categories** is appendix K: every
in-stock line in soap, household, laundry, dishwash, paper, bags, house
wares, bath tools, deodorant and oral care, with its list price, pack and
weight. Any of it can join Tier 0; it is the same supplier on the same terms.

**What Bangalla does NOT have, so these stay in Tier 1:** Swedish dishcloths,
bamboo kitchen tools, wool dryer balls, solid dish soap, seed packets, and
every game.

### 3.2 What "costs nothing" still asks for, before the first order

| | What | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ~~The Dropship Master list~~ **Settled 2026-10-06** | The product data file he downloaded is from Bangalla's "Dropship Master Files" page, which says it "contains all product data and current pricing": the Tier 0 rows are on it |
| 2 | **Bangalla's shipping charge** for a 0.5 lb, a 2 lb and a 7 lb order to an Oklahoma address | He is billed product plus shipping on every order. The store charges the customer $8.95, $12.95 or $18.95 by weight (docs/SHIPPING.md). If Bangalla charges more, the difference comes out of the margin: fill a cart in the account and read the quote without paying |
| 3 | **The daily stock report**, each morning there are orders | Stock moves daily; an order for something sold out is a refund and an apology |
| 4 | **A shelf-price check** on each product before its page | Whether a customer pays Bangalla's list price for a bar of Kirk's is a question about other shops' prices, and nobody has read those yet |
| 5 | **PayPal live, and the money actually available** | The customer pays first, but PayPal can hold a new seller's money for days. The first orders may have to be paid to the supplier from a card before PayPal releases them. That is float, not cost, but it is real |
| 6 | **EIN and the Oklahoma resale certificate** | Both free; Bangalla requires both, Arbico will ask for the certificate |
| 7 | ~~Returns~~ **Answered 2026-10-07** | Bangalla has no policy: each return is asked for and approved or not. Our refund page carries the cost of a refused one |
| 8 | **Two suppliers, two parcels** | A cart with a soap and a bag of worm castings ships from two warehouses and costs two postages. Until the cart splits shipping by supplier, keep the two ranges on separate shelves |
| 9 | **Fertiliser only**: one call to ODAFF | The Fertilizer Act asks anyone distributing fertiliser for a $50 a year licence, and also excludes "a retail store selling only bagged registered commercial fertilizer" from its dealer definition (appendix A, 1.3). Which side a dropshipping web store falls on is theirs to say. Until then the fertiliser rows carry that flag and every other row is clean |
| 10 | **Product photos**: ask each supplier whether its images may be used | Bangalla's file carries an image link for most rows; Arbico's pages have theirs |

### What Tier 0 asks of him

| Step | Cost | Why |
|---|---|---|
| EIN (irs.gov, ten minutes) | $0 | Bangalla requires it |
| Resale certificate, from the sales tax permit already filed | $0 | Both suppliers ask for it |
| Bangalla: confirm the free wholesale level, download the **Dropship Master** and the daily stock report | $0 | Ranks 1 to 19 |
| Bangalla: a filled cart to read the shipping quote; the returns policy in writing | $0 | 3.2, points 2 and 7 |
| [Arbico distributor application](https://www.arbico-organics.com/category/distributor-program-information) to distributors@arbico.com, asking the discount, the Oklahoma restriction list and the shipping charge in one email | $0 | Ranks 20 to 29 |
| ODAFF: does a web store that dropships bagged, registered fertiliser need the $50 licence? | $0 to ask | The fertiliser rows |
| PayPal live keys in Netlify, then `"checkout": "cart"` | $0 | Nothing sells without it (docs/STORE-STEPS.md) |
| Load the Bangalla file into the dashboard's **Store products** section | $0 | Cost, margin and stock beside every row |

## 3A. TIER 1, ranked: the first stock, when there is money for it

**Since 2026-10-06 this is the old Tier 1 (the opening online order) in its
old order, less the 10 lines that turned out to be free to start and
moved to Tier 0.** Still the right first purchase: light, bought again, no
registration of his own. What it needs is money for an opening order, from
$30 (Bentley Seed) to $150 a brand. **Ranks 87 to 96 (2026-10-07, the owner: "use Faire on garden books and
journals") are garden books, journals and kids' titles, grouped by
publisher so one brand minimum covers several.** **The cheapest door when the first money
exists is Faire: 50% off a first order up to $100, net 60 terms and free
returns on an opening order.** Where a row quotes a Bangalla price, it is the
public list price; his cost is in the dashboard.

## 9. What the measurements say about each supplier

**Bangalla** prices soap and personal care by the unit and the eco-home range
by the carton; 20 to 50% of a category is out of stock on a given night; its
garden category holds four products. **Since Tier 0 (2026-10-05) this
stock order is no longer the plan**: the same bars are dropshipped one at a
time, nothing bought ahead. The table is kept as the order that COULD be
placed if he ever wants soap on a shelf of his own. Its prices are the public
list prices; his account pays less. The order as first written:

| Line | Each | Qty | Cost | Units |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kirk's Castile 3-pack | $7.48 | 12 | $89.76 | 12 packs |
| Kirk's Aloe Castile 4 oz | $2.71 | 24 | $65.04 | 24 bars |
| One With Nature Dead Sea 6x4 oz | $22.56 | 4 | $90.24 | 24 bars |
| South of France 6 oz, six scents | $5.41 | 12 | $64.92 | 12 bars |

| Grandpa's Epsom Salt | $5.74 | 6 | $34.44 | 6 bars |
| **Total** | | | **$378.84** | **84 units** |

Check each line's shelf price first and drop any that does not clear a third.

**Faire**: product pages and `discover/<term>` grids are public; wholesale
prices and new-buyer terms need the account; the first order is 50% off up to
$100. Opening minimums read: $30 (Bentley), $50 (Rebel Green, Moss Creek,
Farmer's Daughter, Garden Guru, TBF, Wild Herb), $70 (Humble Suds), $75 (Wild
and Stone, Berkley Green), $85 (MamaSuds), $90 to $150 (most), $200 to $350
(Sunhouse, SuperBee, Rustic Strength, Common Good, Naturally Med, Continuum,
European Soaps).

**GrowOrganic** prints quantity breaks and runs the commercial programme by
email; **Hummert** prints wholesale, SRP and discount in one line; **Seven
Springs** sells sacks at farm prices by the pallet and names its retail
partners; **WebstaurantStore** is the branded case route with no account;
**Azure** stays a member account; **Rustic Strength** and **Fillaree** are the
refill station's makers, both by application; **High Mowing** publishes its

## 11. The order of operations

**Rewritten 2026-10-05: everything that costs nothing comes first.**

1. **EIN**, then the **resale certificate** from the sales tax permit already
   filed. Both free.
2. **Bangalla**: confirm the free wholesale level, download the Dropship
   Master and the daily stock report, read a shipping quote from a filled
   cart, ask for the returns policy. Load the product data file into the
   dashboard. Strike any Tier 0 row not on the Dropship Master.
3. **Arbico** distributor application, with the discount, the Oklahoma list
   and the shipping charge asked in one email.
4. **ODAFF**, one call: the fertiliser licence question.
5. **PayPal live**, and `"checkout": "cart"` (docs/STORE-STEPS.md).
6. A shelf-price check, a photograph and one sentence for each Tier 0 line he
   picks; then the page. Soap first: it is the lightest and the deepest in
   stock.
7. Applications that cost nothing to file and answer later:
   **wholesale@groworganic.com**, Hummert, Rustic Strength, Fillaree,
   Blueland, Meliora, Who Gives A Crap, High Mowing. Each one adds hidden
   prices to the register, and none asks for money until an order.
8. **When the first money exists, Tier 1 in order**: a Faire first order at
   50% off (Three Bluebirds, Bamboo Switch, No Tox Life, Bentley Seed, one
   Outset display), then Hummert's Espoma 4 lb tones, then one
   WebstaurantStore case to prove the numbers, then Frontier's $10
   membership and the games accounts.
9. Tier 4 is a decision about a **room**: scales, labels, jars, a sack. Price
   it when the online store has sold something. Then **Seven Springs** for a
   pallet and **BWI** for the truck.

---

## 12. Regulatory, in one paragraph

Pesticides and devices are registered in Oklahoma by their manufacturers at
$210 a label, including the federally "minimum risk" ones; a retailer checks
the ODAFF lookup and copies the label. Fertiliser under 30 lb is "specialty",
registered by the manufacturer at $100 a product; the $50 annual licence is
his, and three DTE products are barred from the state. Reselling a brand's
cosmetics carries no MoCRA duty; an own label does. Compostable claims are
BPI (industrial) unless a TUV home mark is present. The thirty-day rule
applies to every dropshipped order. De minimis is gone. The full text, with
sources, is appendix A.

Recorded so that a future round does not have to rediscover them, and so a

read, never constructed**: the pattern says where to look, not what exists.

| Supplier | Pattern | Prices without an account? |
|---|---|---|
| ARBICO Organics | `arbico-organics.com/product/<slug>/<category-slug>` | Yes, retail |
| Bangalla | `bangalla.com/<product-slug>/` | **Yes, real wholesale case and single-unit prices** |

## A. The regulatory ground, in full

The five decisions in §1 are the short form of this. Read A before
writing a single product description, and read it again before writing one
for anything that kills a bug or feeds a plant.

This section was researched independently of the three suppliers because it applies to all of them, and because two whole product categories the owner asked for (organic fertilizers and amendments, and pesticides) are regulated product classes in Oklahoma, not ordinary merchandise. None of this is legal advice. It is a list of the rules that exist and where to read them.

#### 1.1 The base requirement: sales tax permit and resale certificate

All three suppliers will want a resale certificate. In Oklahoma a resale certificate can only be issued by a business that already holds a valid Oklahoma Sales Tax Permit, which is applied for through the Oklahoma Tax Commission for a fee in the region of twenty dollars plus handling. Oklahoma accepts the Multistate Tax Commission Uniform Sales and Use Tax Certificate, which is convenient because the same form can be handed to an out of state supplier such as Arbico in Arizona. Suppliers generally want the certificate within ninety days of the sale, and the records should be kept for at least three years.

Practical order of operations: get the sales tax permit first, then the resale certificate, then apply to suppliers. Applying to Bangalla without an EIN and a resale certificate will stall.

Sources: [Oklahoma business licenses and permits](https://oklahoma.gov/business/operate/licenses-and-permits.html), [Oklahoma resale certificate guide](https://taxhero.net/blog/resale-certificate-oklahoma/), [Oklahoma sales tax permit registration](https://handsoffsalestax.com/sales-tax-permit-oklahoma/).

#### 1.2 Pesticides: the rule that catches people out

This is the single most important compliance finding in this report, and it is stronger than the brief assumed.

**Every pesticide sold into Oklahoma must be registered with the State Board of Agriculture.** The Oklahoma pesticide law says, in the section headed PESTICIDE REGISTRATION REQUIRED: "Every pesticide or device distributed, sold, or offered for sale within this state or delivered for transportation or transported in intrastate or interstate commerce shall be registered with the Board." The annual fee is "Two Hundred Ten Dollars ($210.00) for each pesticide or device label registered."

**Who pays that is the good news.** The rules say: "Unless provided otherwise, registrants shall be responsible for the registration of pesticides and devices and may be sanctioned by the Department if unregistered pesticides or devices are distributed, sold or offered for sale within this state ... by registrants, distributors or dealers." The registrant is the company whose name is on the label, that is Monterey, Bonide, Safer, Neudorff and so on. **A retailer does not file the registration and does not pay the $210.** What a retailer must do is make sure the product it lists is in fact registered in Oklahoma, because the rule expressly contemplates the Department sanctioning distributors and dealers who sell unregistered product.

**Three traps worth knowing:**

1. **"Minimum risk" does not mean exempt in Oklahoma.** Many natural pest products (essential oil sprays, some soaps and garlic or rosemary oil products) are exempt from federal EPA registration under FIFRA section 25(b). Oklahoma does not follow that exemption. The rules state: "Pesticides and devices classified under sections 24(c) or 25(b) of the Federal Insecticide, Fungicide, and Rodenticide Act, 7 U.S.C. 136 et seq. shall be registered with the Department and shall comply with all registration requirements established by the Department." So the friendly looking "EPA exempt" natural bug spray still needs an Oklahoma registration behind it. This matches the general national picture, where most states require registration or notification for 25(b) products despite the federal exemption.

2. **"Devices" are regulated too.** Oklahoma defines a device as "any instrument subject to the United States Environmental Protection Agency regulation intended for trapping, destroying, repelling, or mitigating insects or rodents, or mitigating fungi, bacteria, or weeds." That language reaches sticky traps, pheromone traps and similar monitoring products, at the same $210 per label. Any trap listed in the store should be checked the same way a spray is.

3. **Restricted use pesticides need a dealer permit.** "It shall be unlawful for any person to sell, offer for sale, or distribute within this state any restricted use pesticide without first obtaining a restricted use pesticide dealer's permit issued by the Board." The fee is "Fifty Dollars ($50.00) for each location" per year. Nothing in the product lists below is a restricted use pesticide, and an eco store has no reason to carry one, so the simple answer is to stay entirely out of restricted use products and avoid needing the permit at all.

**The practical action:** before listing any pest control product, look it up in Oklahoma's registered product database. ODAFF publishes registered products through Kelly Registration Systems at [kellysolutions.com/ok](https://www.kellysolutions.com/ok/pesticideindex.htm), and there is a newer [ODAFF Online pesticide product search](https://accessodaff.ag.ok.gov/share/YQCkhPWhwK/pesticide/pesticide-product-search). Both need to be opened in a normal browser: the Kelly site refused automated requests during this research and the ODAFF Online search is a JavaScript application, so neither could be queried here. Checking a dozen products by hand is an afternoon of work, not a project.

Sources: [Oklahoma Combined Pesticide Law and Rules, ODAFF](https://ag.ok.gov/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/Oklahoma-Combined-Pesticide-Law-and-Rules.pdf) (quotations above are from the text of that document), [ODAFF restricted use pesticide dealer permit application](https://ag.ok.gov/wp-content/uploads/2023/04/ODAFF-Restricted-Use-Pesticide-Dealer-License.pdf), [EPA minimum risk pesticides](https://www.epa.gov/minimum-risk-pesticides/minimum-risk-pesticide-definition-and-product-confirmation), [ODAFF licensing and permits](https://ag.ok.gov/licensing-permits/).

#### 1.3 Fertilizers and soil amendments: a second registration regime

This was not in the brief and it should have been, because it bites the "organic fertilizers and amendments" category directly.

**Fertilizer.** Under the Oklahoma Fertilizer Act, "Any person engaged in the distribution or sale of fertilizer shall obtain a license", at fifty dollars a year per business location. Products in packages of less than thirty pounds are "specialty fertilizer", and "Registrations for specialty fertilizer products sold in packages of less than thirty (30) pounds shall pay a one-hundred-dollar registration fee for each product", expiring every June 30. Almost everything an eco store would sell is under thirty pounds, so almost everything is specialty fertilizer at $100 per product per year.

**Two exemptions rescue the retailer, and they are worth reading carefully:**

- On product registration: "A distributor shall not be required to register any fertilizer which is already registered under the Oklahoma Fertilizer Act by another person, provided the label does not differ in any respect." So if the brand has registered the product in Oklahoma, the store does not pay $100 again. The condition is that the label is unchanged, which is another argument against private labelling.
- On the licence: the Act's definition of "fertilizer dealer" excludes "a retail store selling only bagged registered commercial fertilizer other than bagged ammonium nitrate". A plain retail store selling other people's already registered bagged product is outside the definition.

**The honest caveat:** that second exemption says "commercial fertilizer", while the under thirty pound goods are defined as "specialty fertilizer". Whether the retail store exclusion covers a store selling only specialty fertilizer, and whether a bottle of liquid fish emulsion counts as "bagged", are exactly the kind of questions that should be put to ODAFF Plant Industry in one phone call rather than guessed at. Do that before the fertilizer category goes live.

**Soil amendments are a separate registration again.** ODAFF requires every soil amendment product to be registered before sale or distribution in Oklahoma, at "$100.00 per product", expiring December 31 each year, and the application demands the label, all advertising material, and "proof of claims" backed by university or independent research. That burden is clearly aimed at the manufacturer, not a shop, but the category is the one that covers worm castings, compost, mycorrhizal inoculants, humic acid and similar. Confirm with ODAFF that the reseller position mirrors the fertilizer one.

Sources: [Oklahoma Fertilizer Act and Rules, ODAFF](https://ag.ok.gov/wp-content/uploads/2021/10/Oklahoma-Fertilizer-Act-Rules.pdf), [OAC 35:30-29-51 fertilizer license and fees](https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/oklahoma/OAC-35-30-29-51), [ODAFF soil amendment registration form and directions](https://ag.ok.gov/wp-content/uploads/2021/05/Soil-Amendment-Registration-Form-1.pdf), [ODAFF Plant Industry](https://ag.ok.gov/plant-industry/).

#### 1.4 Personal care: MoCRA, and the private label trap

Under the Modernization of Cosmetics Regulation Act, facilities that manufacture or process cosmetics must register with FDA, and the "responsible person", defined as the manufacturer, packer or distributor whose name appears on the product label, must list each product, hold safety substantiation records and handle adverse event reports.

**As a pure retailer, Lavish Leaf has no MoCRA registration duty.** Labelers, packagers, holders, distributors and retailers do not register. The moment the company puts its own name on a shampoo bar or a deodorant, it becomes the responsible person and inherits the whole set of obligations. That is the single strongest argument for selling other people's brands at launch and leaving own label for later.

Two product specific notes for the personal care table:

- **Toothpaste and toothpaste tablets containing fluoride are OTC drugs**, not cosmetics, under the anticaries monograph at 21 CFR 355. Fluoride free versions that only claim cleaning are cosmetics. Selling them is fine, but the labelling burden sits with a manufacturer who understands the monograph, which again argues against private label.
- **Deodorant is a cosmetic, antiperspirant is an OTC drug.** This distinction is well established but was not separately source verified in this pass, so confirm before writing any category copy that makes a claim.

Sources: [FDA MoCRA registration and listing overview](https://www.spakinect.com/news/fda-mocra-registration-product-listing-cosmetics), [How MoCRA is reshaping FDA oversight, Foley and Lardner, March 2026](https://www.foley.com/insights/publications/2026/03/how-mocra-is-reshaping-fda-oversight-of-cosmetics-in-2026/), [OTC Monograph M021, anticaries drug products](https://dps-admin.fda.gov/omuf/sites/omuf/files/monograph-documents/2023-05/OTC%20Monograph%20M021-Anticaries%20Drug%20Products%20for%20OTC%20Human%20Use%2005.02.2023.pdf).

#### 1.5 Green claims: what to check, and what not to print

The FTC Green Guides are still the 2012 edition. The FTC asked for comment on an update in 2022 and, as of late 2025, had not issued one, so the old rules govern. Under them an unqualified "degradable" claim requires complete decomposition within a reasonably short period after customary disposal, and "compostable" must be qualified if the product will not break down in a home compost pile or if facilities are not available to most consumers. Selling a product is not the same as repeating its marketing, and the store's own category copy is the store's own liability.

The certifications worth checking, and what they actually mean:

| Mark | What it certifies | Where to verify |
|---|---|---|
| USDA Organic | The NOP standard, applied to agricultural outputs such as food and fibre. Most garden inputs are not eligible for it, so seeing it on a fertilizer bag is usually wrong or refers to an ingredient | [USDA Organic INTEGRITY database](https://organic.ams.usda.gov/integrity/) |
| OMRI Listed | The right mark for inputs. OMRI reviews fertilizers, amendments and pesticides for allowed use in certified organic production. This is the one that matters for the garden category | [OMRI product search](https://www.omri.org/omri-search) |
| EPA registration | A pesticide has been reviewed and carries an EPA registration number. Necessary but not sufficient: Oklahoma registration is separate, see 1.2 | EPA reg number on the label, then the ODAFF lookup |
| Leaping Bunny | No animal testing across the whole supply chain, independently monitored, annual recommitment. "Cruelty free" on its own has no legal meaning in the US | [Leaping Bunny brand directory](https://www.leapingbunny.org/) |
| B Corp | A company level certification, not a product one. A brand claiming it and absent from the directory is a red flag | [B Corp directory](https://www.bcorporation.net/en-us/find-a-b-corp/) |
| BPI Compostable | Industrial composting only. There is no US equivalent of home compostable certification, so a BPI mark does not mean it will rot in a backyard pile | [BPI certified products](https://bpiworld.org/) |
| TUV OK compost HOME | The genuine home compostable mark, European scheme. Rare on US products and worth a premium claim when present | TUV Austria certificate number |

The practical greenwashing rule for this store: a product is only as green as its weakest claim. "Bamboo" with no FSC chain of custody, "biodegradable" with no standard named, and "compostable" without saying home or industrial are the three phrases that should never appear on a Lavish Leaf product page without a certificate behind them.

Sources: [FTC summary of the Green Guides](https://www.ftc.gov/business-guidance/resources/environmental-claims-summary-green-guides), [Green Guides update still pending, Packaging Dive](https://www.packagingdive.com/news/packaging-labeling-recyclable-compostable-green-guides/738514/), [OMRI lists and standards](https://www.omri.org/lists-and-standards), [BPI versus OK compost home](https://holdonbags.com/pages/tuv-bpi-composting-certifications-explained).

#### 1.6 The 30 day rule, which dropshipping breaks quietly

The FTC's Mail, Internet or Telephone Order Merchandise Rule applies to this store and it applies to dropshippers specifically. The requirements, in short:

- Ship within the time advertised. If no time is advertised, ship within 30 days.
- The seller must have a reasonable basis for the time it promises.
- If shipment will be late, notify the customer no later than the originally promised ship date and offer a choice: consent to the delay, or cancel and get a prompt refund.
- Silence can be treated as consent for a first delay of up to 30 days. Longer delays, indefinite delays, and any second delay need actual consent. Without it the money must be refunded without the customer having to ask.

This is the rule that makes long haul dropshipping genuinely risky rather than merely annoying. A product coming from a warehouse in China on a slow economy line, now going through full customs processing, can miss 30 days without anybody doing anything wrong. The store, not the supplier, is the one on the hook. Two defences: publish a realistic shipping window on every product page rather than leaving it blank, and prefer suppliers shipping from US warehouses for anything the store promises quickly.

Source: [FTC, selling on the internet, prompt delivery rules](https://www.ftc.gov/business-guidance/resources/selling-internet-prompt-delivery-rules), [FTC Mail, Internet or Telephone Order Merchandise Rule](https://www.ftc.gov/legal-library/browse/rules/mail-internet-or-telephone-order-merchandise-rule).

#### 1.7 Imports changed in 2025, and it matters for anything from China

The US de minimis exemption, the rule that let commercial parcels under $800 enter duty free with minimal processing, is gone. Executive Order 14324 suspended duty free de minimis treatment for all countries effective 12:01 a.m. Eastern on 2025-08-29. CBP then made the suspension indefinite through two interim final rules, effective 2026-06-24 for shipments outside the postal network and 2026-07-24 for mail.

The practical effect for a dropship store: every low value parcel arriving from abroad now owes applicable duties, taxes and fees, and goes through full customs processing. That adds cost and it adds days, on top of transit. Any margin table built on pre 2025 China dropshipping arithmetic is wrong. This is the single largest reason to treat the third supplier as a limited tool rather than a backbone.

Source: [Federal Register, notice implementing EO 14324 suspending duty free de minimis treatment for all countries](https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2025/09/02/2025-16802/notice-of-implementation-of-the-presidents-executive-order-14324-suspending-duty-free-de-minimis), [Federal Register, indefinite suspension of the de minimis exemption for merchandise arriving through all modes other than the international postal network](https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2026/06/24/2026-12670/indefinite-suspension-of-the-de-minimis-exemption-for-merchandise-arriving-through-all-modes-other), [CBP guidance CSMS 66065494](https://content.govdelivery.com/accounts/USDHSCBP/bulletins/3f01456).

## B. Bangalla: the terms, and every priced case

The only supplier here whose real prices are public before an account
exists, which is why these tables are the backbone of the cleaning and
paper lines above.

**Verdict: the best fit of the three, and the one to build on.** Bangalla is the only supplier here that combines US warehouses, a real dropship programme with no per order fees, brands a customer has already heard of, and a catalogue that maps directly onto what the owner asked for. It also has one significant trap, described in 1H, that would quietly wreck a pricing model if it were not caught.

#### 1A. The terms

Source: [Bangalla dropshipping services](https://www.bangalla.com/dropshipping-services/).

| Term | What Bangalla states |
|---|---|
| Minimum order | None. "No we do not require a minimum order" |
| Per order or handling fee | None. No dropship or handling fees per order, and no monthly minimums |
| Federal EIN or state ID | **Required.** "As a wholesale supplier we do require all businesses to be a company with a State Id or a Federal EIN" |
| Resale certificate | **Required.** Obtained through your state tax department using your federal EIN |
| Live website | Not explicitly required, though the programme is aimed at online merchants |
| Membership tiers | Standard, free, for a basic wholesale account. **Gold**, paid, which adds a 5 percent discount on all items. **Platinum**, paid, which adds FBA fulfilment for Amazon sellers |
| Catalogue size | Over 700 brands across more than 125 categories |

**On the membership fees, be careful.** Bangalla's own dropshipping page describes the three tiers but does not print the prices. Secondary sources circulating publicly quote a Drop Ship Standard at $19.95 setup plus $12.95 a month, and a Platinum at $89.95 setup plus $89.00 a month. **Those figures are secondary and were not confirmed from Bangalla's own current documents.** The official Gold signup PDF could not be read because the host's TLS certificate had expired at the time of this research. Get the current fee schedule from Bangalla in writing before budgeting.

Lavish Leaf already satisfies the hard requirements: it is an Oklahoma corporation, so it has or can get an EIN, and the Oklahoma sales tax permit and resale certificate described in Part 1.1 cover the rest. There is no obvious gate here that a small seller would fail.

#### 1B. Integration

Bangalla provides **Excel and CSV files** to upload products to your own site, plus a **daily stock report** in a Data Downloads area of the account. No API and no platform specific app is mentioned anywhere on the dropshipping page.

That is actually a reasonable fit for the current setup. A CSV plus a daily stock file is exactly what the approach in Part 2.3 wants: keep product data as a structured file in the repo, let the build turn it into pages, and check the daily stock report before fulfilling. It is not real time, and it will not stop the store selling something that went out of stock an hour ago, but for a catalogue of ten to thirty products that is an acceptable risk and it costs nothing in platform fees.

The honest limitation: there is still no order handoff. Orders are keyed into Bangalla by hand. Per Part 2.3, that is fine until it is not.

#### 1C. Shipping

- **Origin:** Bangalla's own US distribution centres. Products under the US dropship programme ship domestically, not from overseas. This is the single biggest practical advantage over CJdropshipping, because none of the customs and de minimis problems in Part 1.7 apply.
- **Handling:** 3 to 6 business days after the order is placed, subject to stock.
- **Transit:** a further 1 to 5 business days depending on destination.
- **To Oklahoma:** that puts a realistic total at roughly **4 to 11 business days**, which is comfortably inside the FTC thirty day rule from Part 1.6, but is slow enough that it should be stated on the product page rather than left blank.
- **Operating hours:** ships Monday to Friday, 10am to 5pm Pacific.
- **Tracking:** provided on all orders.
- **Who pays:** the retailer pays Bangalla for product and shipping, and decides how to present that to the customer. See Part 4.2 before offering free shipping on anything cheap.

#### 1D. Returns

**Could not be verified.** Bangalla's return policy page returned a 404 during this research, and the older bangalladistribution.com terms pages now redirect to a bare domain root. **This is an open item.** Ask Bangalla directly for: the return window, any restocking fee, who pays return freight, the RMA process, and the window for reporting damaged or short shipments. In a dropship arrangement the customer contacts the store first regardless, so the store will be coordinating every return whatever the policy says.

#### 1E. Blockers for a small seller

Nothing here is disqualifying, but four things are worth knowing:

- **MAP policy could not be confirmed.** Many of the brands Bangalla carries, particularly Seventh Generation, Mrs. Meyer's and Method, are mainstream brands whose owners commonly enforce minimum advertised pricing. Ask before setting prices.
- **The catalogue is mostly other people's brands, sold everywhere.** Seventh Generation is in every grocery store in America. Competing on price with Target is not a strategy. The differentiation has to come from curation, from the farm and compost connection, and from local service, not from the products themselves.
- **Handling time of 3 to 6 business days is slow** by 2026 expectations, before transit even starts.
- **The public pricing is misleading.** See the next section, which is the most important thing in this part of the report.

#### 1F. Certification notes for this catalogue

The good news is that most of the compliance burden sits with brands that have been doing this for decades. Per Part 1.4, Lavish Leaf as a pure retailer has no MoCRA registration duty for any personal care item it resells, and per Part 1.5 the brand owner carries its own substantiation for its own claims.

Points to check per brand rather than assume:

- **Leaping Bunny and B Corp** are brand level and easy to verify in the public directories linked in Part 1.5. Several brands in this catalogue plausibly hold one or both. Verify rather than repeat.
- **BioBag and the compostable tableware lines** (Repurpose, World Centric) make compostability claims. Per Part 1.5, check whether the certification is **BPI industrial** or genuinely home compostable, and qualify the claim on the product page accordingly. Most will be industrial only.
- **"Recycled" claims** on the paper lines (Seventh Generation, Green Forest) are usually well substantiated and carry a stated percentage. Reproduce the percentage rather than saying "recycled".
- **Disinfecting products are pesticides.** The Seventh Generation disinfecting wipes and disinfecting cleaners, and the Cleanwell disinfecting wipes, make antimicrobial claims, which means they are EPA registered antimicrobial pesticides. Per Part 1.2 they need Oklahoma registration behind them like any other pesticide. These are easy to list without thinking about it, because they look like ordinary cleaning products. They are not.
- Nothing in the household range is a fertilizer or soil amendment, so Part 1.3 does not apply to this supplier.

Earth Breeze and Bee's Wrap in particular are the two the owner's brief points at most directly. Pull their case prices when the account is open.

#### 1H. The pricing trap, and it is a serious one

**Answered 2026-10-05.** His account's product data file settles it: the
price Bangalla shows the public is its LIST price, and an account pays less.
The examples below are therefore list prices, as this section suspected. His
real costs are in the dashboard (private), because this repository is public.

Bangalla displays case prices publicly, which is unusual and looks like a gift. **Divide them out and they do not behave like wholesale prices.** Worked examples from the tables above:

| Product | Computed per unit from the public case price | Common US retail |
|---|---|---|
| Seventh Generation Free and Clear Ultra Liquid Laundry, 100 oz | $26.95 | roughly $18 |
| Seventh Generation Disinfecting Wipes, 35 ct | $8.70 | roughly $5 to $7 |
| Mrs. Meyer's dryer sheets, 80 count | $15.00 | roughly $7 to $10 |
| If You Care household gloves, one pair | $6.29 | roughly $5 to $7 |
| Bon Ami Polish and Cleanser, 14 oz | $3.42 | roughly $2.79 to $3.99 |

A wholesale price that sits at or above shelf price is not a wholesale price. Bangalla's own eco home page states that only approved accounts can access products and sub categories in that area, so the most likely explanation is that **logged out visitors are shown a list or reference price, and approved accounts see something lower.** A second possibility is that these are accurate case prices for a small distributor and the margins on mainstream brands really are thin, in which case the conclusion for the store is the same.

Either way the practical instruction is identical and it is important:

**Do not build any pricing model, margin table or product page from the prices visible on Bangalla's public pages.** Open the account, pull the real CSV and the daily stock file, and rebuild the numbers from those. This report deliberately does not publish a margin column for Bangalla, because computing one from observed public prices would produce numbers that look authoritative and are probably wrong.

What can be said with confidence, from Part 4.1: expect somewhere between 25 and 40 percent off retail through a distributor dropship arrangement, favour the concentrated and powdered formats where the shipping weight per use is lowest, and be realistic that mainstream brands sold in every grocery store will carry the thinnest margins in the catalogue. The specialist brands, Charlie's Soap, Bi-O-Kleen, Bio-Pac, BioBag, Earth Breeze, Bee's Wrap, are where both the margin and the differentiation are more likely to live.

#### 1I. What could not be verified for Bangalla

1. **The current membership fee schedule.** The tiers are named on Bangalla's own page but not priced. The public figures quoted in 1A are secondary, and the official signup PDF could not be opened because its host's TLS certificate had expired.
2. **The return policy in full.** The return policy URL returned a 404 and the legacy domain redirects to a bare root. Window, restocking fee, return freight and RMA process are all unknown.
3. **Real approved account pricing**, which is the central open question. See 1H.
4. **MAP policy**, which is likely to exist for the mainstream brands.
5. **Whether the dropship programme sells single units at a per unit price**, or whether the case pack shown is the purchase unit. For a dropship programme it must be the former, but that was not stated on any page that could be read.
6. **Whether Bangalla blind ships** under the retailer's name. Not stated on the pages read.
7. **Case pack sizes for four products** where the display omitted them, marked "not stated" in the tables.
8. **Some catalogue data quality issues.** Several Earth Friendly Dishmate variants display as "1 x 25 oz" at the same price as the 6 x 25 oz cases, which is almost certainly a display error rather than real pricing.
9. **Earth Breeze and Bee's Wrap pricing**, confirmed as carried brands but not reached in this pass.

## C. Arbico: the terms, the Oklahoma shipping bans, and the 2026-09-20 lines

Read before the distributor application is posted. **C includes the
state-by-state shipping restrictions**, which is the paragraph that changed
the garden list in §3.1.

**Verdict: usable, and better than expected, but not as a plain static storefront.** Arbico runs a genuine distributor dropship program with terms a small seller would struggle to beat. The problems are not commercial, they are regulatory and technical, and one of them is severe enough to change the website plan.

#### 2A. The terms

Arbico operates a formal **Distributor Program**, distinct from its affiliate scheme, and it is built around dropshipping. Source: [Arbico distributor program information](https://www.arbico-organics.com/category/distributor-program-information).

| Term | What the public page states |
|---|---|
| Dropship fee | None. The page says there are no additional dropship fees |
| Minimum order | None, no minimums stated |
| Case break fee | None |
| Blind shipping | Yes, shipped under your brand to your customer |
| Inventory required | None, including for live insects |
| How to apply | Download a distributor application PDF, return it by email to distributors@arbico.com or by fax |
| Sales channel restriction | Your own website, social media and privately managed channels only. **Amazon, Walmart, Alibaba and other third party marketplaces are explicitly prohibited** |
| Geography | US domestic addresses only |
| Discount tiers, annual minimum, resale certificate, MAP | **Not published.** These live in the application PDF, which was not publicly reachable |

Those four unpublished terms are the ones that decide whether this is a business. A resale certificate will almost certainly be required, because otherwise Arbico would have to charge sales tax. Everything else needs one email to distributors@arbico.com before any plan is built on it.

Two things to ignore: a coupon aggregator advertising "save up to 15 percent" refers to retail quantity breaks on the consumer site, not distributor pricing. And the separate [affiliate program](https://www.arbico-organics.com/category/affiliate-program) at 7.5 percent commission is closed to new applicants.

#### 2B. Integration: there is none

No product feed, no CSV export, no API, no platform app. Nothing in the distributor page, the affiliate page or anywhere else on the site. The only bulk product data Arbico publishes is a seasonal PDF catalogue, most recently Spring 2026, in an [archive going back to 2018](https://www.arbico-organics.com/category/catalog-archive).

Every SKU is hand keyed: name, size variants, description, price, image, and a per SKU state restriction list. Budget ten to twenty minutes per SKU to do it properly, so a fifty SKU catalogue is a ten to fifteen hour build. That is tolerable. Three things that follow from it are not:

- **Prices move and a static page goes stale silently.** During this research a site wide "Fall Beneficial Nematode Sale, up to 30 percent off" was running from 2026-08-28 to 2026-09-30, so every nematode carries a list price and a sale price, and those sale prices expire in ten days.
- **Stock status is invisible.** At read time Arbico's wild collected ladybugs, Trichogramma pretiosum, Alfalfa Meal 4 lb, Glacial Rock Dust, Volcanic Rock Dust and both Impact Organics castings sizes were out of stock, and praying mantid egg cases cannot ship at all in 2026 because of an APHIS restriction stated on their own page. A static page will happily sell every one of them.
- **There is no order handoff.** Every order becomes a manual re-key into Arbico's system or a phone call.

#### 2C. The finding that changes the website plan

Arbico applies **per SKU state shipping bans**, and they are extensive. These are not hazmat rules. Arbico explains them plainly: the manufacturer chose not to register that product in that state, usually because the annual state fee is not worth it for the volume. Source: [why some products have shipping restrictions](https://www.arbico-organics.com/why_restrictions). This is the same state registration regime described in Part 1.2 and 1.3, viewed from the supplier's side.

Verified examples as of 2026-09-20:

| Product | Cannot ship to |
|---|---|
| Fertilome Come And Get It II Fire Ant Killer | 33 jurisdictions including CA, NY, IL, PA, OH, MI, WA |
| Down To Earth Garden Gypsum | 26 jurisdictions **including Oklahoma** |
| Perma-Guard Crawling Insect DE | 19 states |
| Down To Earth Granular Humic Acids | 15 jurisdictions **including Oklahoma** |
| Monterey Insecticidal Soap RTU | AK, DC, HI, ME, MT, ND, SC, SD, WY |
| Down To Earth All Purpose 4-6-2 | IA, ND, **Oklahoma**, WV, WY |
| Actinovate SP | DC, AZ, PR |
| Monterey 70 percent Neem Oil RTU | WY |

**Three of those cannot be shipped into Oklahoma at all**, which means Lavish Leaf could not sell them to a customer in its own home state. That is the fertilizer and soil amendment registration regime from Part 1.3 biting in practice, and it is direct evidence that the regime is enforced rather than theoretical.

There is no honest way to express this on a static HTML page. A per SKU, per state rule has to be enforced at checkout, or the store will take orders it cannot fill and then have to cancel them. This is the strongest argument in the whole report for finishing the existing Netlify Functions cart rather than hand coding product pages: the restriction logic belongs in the function.

#### 2D. Shipping

Source: [Arbico shipping information](https://www.arbico-organics.com/category/shipping-info).

- **Origin:** Oro Valley, Arizona, in the Tucson metro.
- **Carriers:** USPS, FedEx and UPS. FedEx and UPS to physical addresses only, USPS for PO boxes.
- **Processing:** 24 to 48 hours excluding weekends.
- **Transit to Oklahoma:** **ESTIMATE, two to three business days by ground.** Arbico publishes no zone map or transit chart, so this is inferred from standard ground zones, not their figure.
- **Free shipping** is a per product flag, not an order value threshold. There are separate free ground, free two day and free overnight tiers attached to individual products, lower 48 only. Source: [free shipping category](https://www.arbico-organics.com/category/free-shipping).
- **Surcharges:** $7.50 for signature required, $20 to upgrade a free shipping item to overnight, and orders over 150 lb become freight requiring a quote.
- **Who pays:** the customer, unless the specific product carries a free shipping flag.

**Live insects run on their own timetable**, and this is the hardest part of the catalogue to operate:

| Regime | Products | Rules |
|---|---|---|
| Tuesday only, overnight | Green lacewing larvae, Stratiolaelaps, Aphidius colemani, Amblyseius cucumeris, two spotted ladybugs | FedEx Priority Overnight, ships Tuesdays only, six to seven day lead time, Tuesday noon cutoff, must be used within 4 to 18 hours of receipt |
| Multi day, ground or two day | Lacewing eggs and cards, nematodes, Encarsia formosa, Fly Eliminators | Monday to Thursday or Monday to Friday, often free USPS or second day air |

Nematodes have a fourteen day refrigerated shelf life and arrive with a frozen ice pack. Cancelling or modifying a scheduled live shipment needs seven business days notice. Nearly every live product excludes HI, PR, VI, GU, AS, PW and AK.

The [live shipment guarantee](https://www.arbico-organics.com/category/live-shipment-guarantee) promises live arrival and a resend, but requires a report within 24 hours with photos or video, requires that the product not be discarded, and **excludes shipments left unattended at the delivery address**. In a dropship model the store has no control over a customer's porch, so that exclusion is the largest live goods liability in the catalogue.

#### 2E. Returns

Source: [Arbico return policy](https://www.arbico-organics.com/category/return-policy).

- 30 days from purchase, unused, unopened, undamaged, original packaging only.
- **20 percent restocking fee** on items that shipped with free shipping.
- Customer pays return shipping. Original shipping is not refundable. Refused packages have freight both ways deducted.
- RMA required in advance from Customer Care.
- **No returns on live product, ever.** Only the 24 hour viability claim.
- Opened pesticides are not returnable, which is normal and legally sensible.
- Clearance, closeout and discontinued items are final sale.

Arbico handles the RMA, but in a blind dropship model the customer contacts the store first, so the store absorbs the coordination. Note the arithmetic: a returned free shipping item costs the 20 percent restocking fee plus the original outbound freight plus the customer's return freight. On a typical garden margin that is a net loss on any return, which is an argument for a generous but carefully worded returns page rather than a permissive one.

#### 2F. Other blockers

- **Marketplace prohibition.** No Amazon, no Walmart. If growth was ever going to run through marketplaces, this supplier forecloses it.
- **Freight and dimensional weight.** The Compost Wizard Dueling Tumbler and Soil Machine PRO are oversize, ground only, no expedited option, and ship assembled. Tank's Organic Compost requires a phone order and a manual shipping quote. Rice hulls and coir blocks are dimensional weight exposure. See Part 4.3.
- **Hazmat** is a low concern here. No explicit hazmat or ORM-D restrictions were found, and ORM-D was phased out by USDOT in 2021. Surround WP is flagged ground only, which is the nearest thing to a hazmat signal on the site.
- **MAP policy is unpublished.** Ask before setting any price.

#### 2G. What the certifications on these pages actually say

Worth reading carefully, because Arbico uses "OMRI Listed" heavily in general marketing copy while individual product pages differ.

- For nearly everything Arbico sells, **OMRI Listed is the correct claim and USDA Organic is not**. Writing "USDA Organic" on a bottle of neem oil would be a false claim. See Part 1.5.
- Two adjacent marks appear: **CDFA Registered Organic Input Material**, California's state input programme, on most Down To Earth products, and **WSDA Approved**, Washington's, on Entrust SC and Surround WP. Both are legitimate input programmes. Neither is USDA Organic.
- Some pages say "NOP compliant", which is a statement about use in organic systems, not a certification. Reproduce that wording verbatim or not at all.
- **Copy the claim only from the exact SKU page.** Several products, including Bonide Liquid Copper, Bonide Sulfur, Mosquito Dunks and Come And Get It II, carried no OMRI badge on their own page despite the site wide copy.
- Fly Eliminators says "USDA approved". That is neither USDA Organic nor an EPA registration. Do not upgrade it.
- **Arbico does not print EPA registration numbers in page text**, only in linked manufacturer label PDFs. So in the tables below, EPA registration is recorded as "not stated on page" throughout, and any note about a product being a registered pesticide is an inference from the active ingredient. That is an absence of page text, not evidence either way.

#### 2H. On prices and margin in the tables below

**Arbico publishes no wholesale or distributor cost anywhere.** Not on the distributor page, not in the catalogues. Every price in the tables is **retail, read from an individual Arbico product page on 2026-09-20**, unless flagged otherwise. There is therefore no margin column, because inventing one would be guessing.

For modelling only, assume a garden trade distributor discount of **30 to 50 percent off retail. That is an assumption, not data.** Per Part 4.1, the lower end of that band is the more realistic planning number. The real figure arrives with the distributor application.

#### 2J. What could not be verified for Arbico

1. **Distributor discount tiers, annual minimums, resale certificate requirement and any membership fee.** The application PDF was not publicly reachable. These four terms decide the business case.
2. **MAP policy.** Nothing published.
3. **Whether a dropship reseller carries the Oklahoma fertilizer tonnage reporting duty**, or whether it stays with the registrant.
4. **Transit time from Arizona to Oklahoma.** No zone map published, so the two to three day figure is an estimate.
5. **Live goods remedy type.** The guarantee page does not say whether the remedy is replacement or refund.
6. **Weather and seasonal hold policy.** Not published, although products do go out of season.
7. **EPA registration numbers for any product.** Not printed in page text.
8. **Whether Oklahoma's device registration reaches sticky traps and pheromone traps.**
9. **Azomite Granulated price and restrictions**, taken from a category listing rather than a product page.
10. **Items Arbico does not appear to carry** that were on the wanted list: Ladbrooke soil blockers, standalone greensand, standalone seedling heat mats and humidity domes, backpack sprayers, and Japanese beetle bag traps. Greensand appears only as an ingredient in blends.

## D. The Down To Earth refusal, and Arbico’s distributor programme

### The DTE application was refused, and Arbico is the way in

**Updated 2026-09-27.** He applied to Down To Earth for a retailer account
and was **denied**, so the whole of §1.1 below (the catalogue, the state
registration list, the wholesale telephone number) is now background rather
than a plan. What replaces it:

**ARBICO Organics carries the Down To Earth line and runs a distributor
programme**, read off their own pages on 2026-09-27:

> "No additional dropship fees, no minimums, no case break fees." Live
> products are drop shipped directly to your customers, and products are
> "blind shipped to your customers to maintain your brand".

* **How to apply:** a Distributor Application PDF on
  `arbico-organics.com/category/distributor-program-information`, emailed to
  **distributors@arbico.com** or faxed to 520-825-2038. Telephone
  **800-827-2847**.
* **The restriction that matters, and it suits him:** resale is allowed on
  "a distributors’ website, social media, and other privately managed
  channels" and **not** on Amazon, Walmart, Alibaba or other marketplaces. He
  sells on lavishleaf.org, so this costs him nothing.
* **US domestic shipping only.**
* **Discounts are not published**; they come with approval. Every price below
  is therefore Arbico’s **retail** price, read from their category pages on
  2026-09-27, and is the ceiling rather than his cost.

**What this changes about the registration problem:** he is not the
registrant and never was. Buying through a distributor who already sells into
Oklahoma moves the fertiliser-registration question off his desk for anything
Arbico already ships there. The $50 annual Oklahoma **licence** for a person
who distributes fertiliser still applies to him; the $100-per-product
registration is the manufacturer’s.

> **Neem is where the pesticide line is, and Arbico’s listings show it.** The
> BONIDE, Monterey, AzaSol, AzaGuard, Molt-X and Rango products are
> **registered pesticides**, which is why they may make pest claims. DTE’s own
> **Neem Seed Meal 6-1-2** is sold as a soil amendment and makes none. In
> Oklahoma a pesticide label costs **$210 a year to register** and the
> registrant is the manufacturer, not the shop: selling a registered product
> is ordinary retail, and **writing a pest-control claim onto a product that
> is not registered for it is the thing that is not.** Sell the neem, copy the
> manufacturer’s words, and add nothing.

## F. The complete Down To Earth line, the fourteen, and the alternatives

Background rather than a plan, since the DTE application was refused: the
full 66-product catalogue is here because it is what the range is chosen
FROM, and the $6,600 arithmetic is what makes the registration question
worth understanding.

### The complete Down To Earth line

He asked for all of it in one place to judge. This is the full catalogue as DTE publishes it today, with the analyses they state. Package sizes: **compostable boxes up to 15 lb, bags above 15 lb**, pouches and bags for the biologicals and wettable powders, and 20 lb tubs for the two Root Zone granulars.

**Blends (17)**: Acid Mix 4-3-6 · All Purpose 4-6-2 · Bio-Fish 7-7-2 · Bio-Live 5-4-2 · Bio-Live Hawaiian Blend · Bio-Turf 8-3-5 · Cactus & Succulent 8-3-5 · Citrus Mix 6-3-3 · Flower Garden 6-3-3 · Fruit Tree 6-2-4 · Houseplant 8-3-1 · Palm Tree 6-2-8 · Rose & Flower Mix 4-8-4 · Starter Mix 3-3-3 · Tree & Shrub 4-2-4 · Vegan Mix 3-2-2 · Vegetable Garden 4-4-4

**Single ingredients (23)**: Alfalfa Meal 2-0-1 · Azomite granular · Azomite powder · Bat Guano 7-3-1 · Blood Meal 12-0-0 · Bone Meal 3-15-0 · Cottonseed Meal 6-2-1 · Crab Meal 4-3-0 · Feather Meal 12-0-0 · Fish Bone Meal 4-12-0 · Fish Meal 8-6-0 · Granular Humic Acids · Greensand/Greenstone Metabasalt · Insect Frass · Island Phosphate · Kelp Meal 1-0.1-2 · Langbeinite 0-0-22 · Neem Seed Meal 6-1-2 · Oyster Shell · Rock Phosphate 0-3-0 · Seabird Guano 0-11-0 · Seaweed Meal · Shrimp Meal 6-6-0

**Minerals (4)**: Garden Sulfur · Dolomite Lime · Garden Gypsum · Garden Lime

**Wettable powders (7)**: AGmino 14-0-0 · Calcium 96 · Fish Bone Powder 4-20-0 · Fish Powder 12-1-1 · Gypsum · Langbeinite · Potassium Sulfate

**Liquids (5)**: All Purpose 4-1-3 · Bloom 2-6-4 · Bone Meal 0-12-0 · Calcium 5% · Fish & Kelp 2-4-1

**Biologicals / mycorrhizae (6)**: Granular Root Zone · Hawaiian Blend Granular Root Zone · Hawaiian Root Growth Enhancer · Granular Root Growth Enhancer · Soluble Root Zone · Soluble Root Growth Enhancer

**Potting media (4)**: Coconut Coir Fiber · Coconut Husk Chips · DTE All Natural Potting Soil · Pro-Organic Mix

**Bundles (4, listed as coming soon)**: Beginner's Garden · Backyard Bounty · Patio Paradise · Tasty Tomatoes

> **66 products.** At Oklahoma's **$100 per specialty-fertiliser product per year** (anything under 30 lb), listing the whole line would cost **$6,600 a year in registrations before a single sale**, if the registrations were yours to file. They are not: the registrant is Down To Earth, and DTE publishes a **"Fertilizer Registration by State"** list for exactly this. **Action: get that list and stock only what is already registered in Oklahoma.** That one document decides this entire category.

### The fourteen, by what a gardener comes in asking for

Not 66 products. Fourteen, which cover every real request:

| The ask | Product | Why this one |
|---|---|---|
| **Nitrogen, fast** | Blood Meal 12-0-0 | The standard answer, and the one people name |
| **Nitrogen, slow** | Feather Meal 12-0-0 | Same number, months not weeks. Sell the pair together |
| **Nitrogen, vegan** | Alfalfa Meal 2-0-1 | The only credible vegan N, and a growth-hormone story people like |
| **Phosphorus** | Bone Meal 3-15-0 | The default |
| **Phosphorus, vegan/marine** | Fish Bone Meal 4-12-0 or Seabird Guano 0-11-0 | One of the two, not both |
| **Potassium** | Langbeinite 0-0-22 | Highest K in the line, no pH swing |
| **Potassium, gentle** | Kelp Meal 1-0.1-2 | Trace minerals; sells on story as much as analysis |
| **Everything in one box** | Vegetable Garden 4-4-4 | The single best-selling shape of product in this category |
| **Everything, no animals** | Vegan Mix 3-2-2 | Growing demand, almost no competition locally |
| **Tomatoes** | Rose & Flower 4-8-4 | The bloom-booster slot |
| **Acid lovers** | Acid Mix 4-3-6 | Blueberries, azaleas: a question asked every spring |
| **Houseplants** | Liquid All Purpose 4-1-3 | The only liquid worth stocking first: it is the year-round one |
| **Mycorrhizae** | Granular Root Zone (+ Soluble Root Zone) | See below |
| **Minerals** | Garden Lime and Garden Gypsum | Two, not four |

**Mycorrhizae, in plain terms, because it is the highest-margin line in the whole garden category:** a 1 lb pouch retails around $25–35 and weighs nothing. DTE's Root Zone products are the granular (mix into the planting hole) and the soluble (water it in). Stock **one of each**, plus a 20 lb tub only if he sells at markets.

### Cheaper alternatives to Down To Earth

DTE is the quality name and the one he wants. These are the real competitors, cheapest first:

| Instead of | Alternative | The trade |
|---|---|---|
| DTE blends | **Jobe's Organics** | Cheapest branded organic, widest big-box distribution. Lower perceived quality, granules not meals |
| DTE blends | **Espoma** (Plant-tone, Garden-tone, Holly-tone) | The best-known name in organic fertiliser in America and usually cheaper per pound. **Espoma is the safe commercial choice; DTE is the differentiated one** |
| DTE blends | **Dr. Earth** | Similar price to DTE, similar positioning, easier retail distribution |
| DTE single ingredients | **Any feed-grade or ag-grade 50 lb bag, repacked** | Blood meal, bone meal, kelp meal and alfalfa are commodities. A 50 lb sack bought from a farm supply and repacked into 2 lb kraft bags is **the single biggest margin available in this document**, and it makes YOU the registrant, with the $100 per product per year and the label law that goes with it. Do not do this until the fertiliser licence is in hand and he has read the labelling rules |
| DTE mycorrhizae | **Wildroot Organic, Plant Success, MYKOS** | All three sell wholesale and are cheaper per pouch |

**Recommendation:** open the DTE wholesale account (application at downtoearthdistributors.com, or 541-485-5932; a resale ID is required and prices are only visible after approval), and carry **Espoma as the value tier beneath it**. Two tiers sell better than one.

### The rest of the garden list he asked for

**Compost tea.** Two shapes: the brewer (a 5-gallon bucket kit, heavy, dropship only) and the **sachets/tea bags**, which are light, repeat-purchase and high-margin. Stock the sachets; list the brewer as special order.

**Water / chlorine filter.** This is a genuinely under-served ask and the margins are good because it is small and dry:
- A **garden-hose carbon filter** (screw-on, replaceable cartridge): the product most people actually want, and the cartridge is the repeat sale.
- **Vitamin C (ascorbic acid) dechlorination tablets**: dissolve in a watering can; neutralise both chlorine and chloramine; pennies per dose, weightless, and almost nobody stocks them.
- A **shower-head filter** bridges into the household side of the store.

**Composting.** Split by weight:
- *Light, sell online:* countertop **compost caddy** (1–2 gal), **charcoal filters** for it (a repeat sale, 2–4 a year), **compostable liner bags** (repeat), **compost thermometer**, **bokashi bran** (repeat) and bokashi bucket kits.
- *Heavy, dropship or in person:* tumblers, three-bay wooden bins, worm farms. Carry **one** tumbler and **one** worm farm so the range looks complete, and let the supplier ship them.

**Beneficial insects and natural pest control.** The 09-20 research is emphatic and it is right: **Oklahoma requires state registration for pesticides including the federally exempt "minimum risk" ones, and for devices such as traps, at $210 per label per year, paid by the registrant.** So:
- **Diatomaceous earth**: food grade, sold as a soil amendment or absorbent rather than with pest claims, is the ordinary way this is retailed. The moment the listing says "kills insects" it is a pesticide claim and the product must be Oklahoma-registered. **Write the listing carefully.**
- **Neem**: two different products, and the difference matters: **neem seed MEAL** (a fertiliser, already in the DTE line above) and **neem OIL spray** (a pesticide, needs registration).
- **Insect frass**: in the DTE line, sold as a fertiliser.
- **Live beneficial insects** (ladybirds, lacewings, nematodes): Arbico ships them, they are perishable, they arrive alive or they are a refund, and they are **not** a line for a one-person store to start with. Revisit at volume.

---

## G. The household refill system, in product-type detail

The form to stock each thing in, which is the part of the older research
that the tiers above compress. Where it names a form (tablets, a bar, a
replaceable head) that form is the recommendation.

This is the part of the store that can actually work by post, and his instinct about restocking is the whole business model.

### The refill shape

**Sell the empty once, sell the refill for ever.**

- **Starter kit:** 3 or 4 labelled glass or aluminium bottles with trigger sprays, plus one concentrate or one tablet of each. Retail $35–55. This is the acquisition product.
- **Refills:** concentrate bottles, tablets, powders or pouches. Retail $5–15 each, ship for almost nothing. This is the profit.
- **Bulk refills:** gallon jugs and 5 lb pouches for the customer who is already converted. Lower margin per ounce, much larger basket.

### The cleaning line, by the FORM to stock each thing in

| Product | Form to stock | Why |
|---|---|---|
| All-purpose cleaner | **Tablets** (drop in water) | Lightest possible way to ship a cleaner: a strip of tablets is grams, a bottle of cleaner is pounds |
| Glass cleaner | Tablets | Same |
| Bathroom cleaner | Tablets or concentrate | Same |
| Dish soap | **Solid block** or powder | A dish block outlasts three plastic bottles and ships flat |
| **Dishwasher powder** | Bulk tub + refill pouch | Powder beats pods on price and on plastic; pods are wrapped in PVA film, which is the thing his customers are avoiding |
| **Laundry powder** | Bulk tub + refill pouch | The anchor product of the whole store: everybody buys it, monthly |
| Laundry stain bar | Bar | Weightless, impulse buy at the till |
| Wool dryer balls | Set of 6 | Replaces dryer sheets, lasts years, excellent margin, light |
| Scouring powder | Shaker tin | Cheap to source, high mark-up |
| Castile soap | Gallon + small bottles | Dr. Bronner's is the name people search for and it refills everything else on this list |

### Personal care (his list, plus what belongs with it)

| Product | Note |
|---|---|
| **Charcoal toothpaste** | Ask for it by name; stock **tablets** as well as the tube, because tablets are plastic-free and ship at nothing |
| **Bamboo toothbrushes** | The single best-known plastic-free product there is. Stock 4-packs, and a family pack |
| **Natural deodorant** | Cardboard push-up tubes, not plastic. Two or three scents plus unscented; unscented sells more than people expect |
| **Shampoo and conditioner bars** | Not bottles. A bar is 2 oz and replaces two 12 oz bottles |
| **Body wash** | A bar, or a concentrate for anybody who insists on liquid |
| Safety razor + blades | High margin, and the **blades are the repeat sale** |
| Silk/bamboo floss in a refillable glass dispenser | Repeat refill |
| Cotton rounds, reusable | Pairs with everything above |
| Soap dishes, wooden | Attach to every bar sold |

### Things that replace plastic in a home

Recycle and storage bins, which he asked for, and the rest of the obvious set: **beeswax wraps**, **stainless or silicone food storage**, **glass jars with bamboo lids**, **produce bags**, **Swedish dishcloths** (one replaces ~15 rolls of paper towel and is the best margin-per-gram item in the store), **bamboo dish brushes with replaceable heads** (the head is the repeat sale), **wool or coconut scrubbers**, **compostable bin liners**, **stainless straws**, **reusable coffee filters**.

**On recycle bins specifically:** stackable sorting bins are bulky, cheap and ship badly. Carry **collapsible** ones, and otherwise treat bins as a market-stall and local-delivery line.

### What NOT to stock, in the 09-27 wording

Say no to these and the store reads as considered rather than random: clothing and textiles (sizes, returns, dead stock), anything electronic, bamboo cutlery sets (bought once, never again), "eco" gadgets with no refill, and anything whose only claim is the word bamboo on a plastic product.
